  curled in either. A real effect of the counting and a small one; whether it touches the far larger damage of the
  openings is untested, and what that damage is has not yet been read from the wiring.
- **Time.** Flat four-direction space under "time keeps energy" does not re-curl from a push of 128; a scar of about 20
  points holds the push. The moving band of Update 36 is not seen at this size, and the speed test (T47 part A) showed the
  steady fronts any uniform medium gives.
- **What this does to the thesis.** Claim 4's shape stands where it was shown, in two dimensions: a specific arrangement
  opening sharply from a seed with an exact release. Beyond two dimensions, in this family at these settings, the
  mechanism lacks a way to open without damaging what opens. Candidates, each to be decided under S1 before any run:
  interchangeable points in the opening runs (T42's lever, smaller than first written; the knob is already decided,
  Update 12); pricing the true barrier exactly before choosing pushes; a different model (Update 28). None of this is about the real universe; each
  result says what this family can and cannot do.

Update 38 (2026-09-27, 12:45 ET): **what a fully curled X is in this model, exactly, and the question it puts to the
author.** Why this page changes: Update 37 said the openings' release damaged a quarter or more of the new space. Read
from the saved wiring after the author asked what "melted" means, that was wrong in kind, and the reason is structural
(ASSUMPTIONS O85).

- **Exact** (*ours, applying a published theorem on graphs [Mul79], to verify by reading its proof*): an arrangement
  with every direction curled at every point can be one connected piece only up to 4^D points (64 with six links, 256 with
  eight), and at that size it is the hypercube. So in this family a fully curled X larger than one cube is a gas of
  separate pieces.
- **Read:** from a gas, no opening made a space larger than one cube. Each cube opened, or partly opened, on its own, and
  the "damage" was the joints between cubes and the seams between opened and curled parts. From a connected torus, one
  region opened with a seam beside it. Update 37's "damages a quarter or more" is corrected there.
- **The question for the author.** Is X one connected thing with at least one direction already open (which the model
  cannot name; in four directions, three curled beside one open), or a foam of many tiny fully curled pieces that open
  separately and must then join? The model allows both; only the first has been seen to open into one region. T48 (a
  connected torus, with released energy kept where it is released, the setting in which the two-dimensional tube opened as
  a front) and T49 (one cube: does it open all its directions, and in what order?) are pre-registered and running.

Update 39 (2026-09-27, 15:50 ET): **the author's answers on what X may be.** Why this page changes: Update 38 asked
whether X is one connected thing with a direction already open or a foam of tiny fully curled pieces, and the runs of
T48 and T49 depend on it. Her words, put in order.

- **X may start with one direction already open**, "if that makes things work." So the connected tori of T48 (in three
  directions, one or two curled beside one open; in four, three curled beside one open) are within her picture, not a
  compromise of it. The model still cannot say which direction is time.
- **Points may be interchangeable before, after, or both.** *Ours:* this opens a knob not yet defined: the counting of
  renamings applied in X only, in space only, or throughout. Its exact form is fixed in writing before any run (S1);
  the runs so far use one treatment throughout.
- **The three space directions and the fourth may be tied evenly or unevenly.** *Ours:* the tie of Update 30 in any
  shape (O70) already allows this in four directions; T46 ran one uneven form.
- **The foam: "could be?"** She had not considered it and holds it open. Recorded as a candidate for X, not adopted:
  X as many tiny fully curled pieces that open separately and must then join. *Ours:* in this model the pieces opened
  but never joined (O85); whether any rule lets them join is the test that would make the foam a live candidate, and it
  is designed after T49 reads.
- **Her predictions**, recorded before any result was read: T48, the first direction opens and the last stays curled
  (she now leans this way); T50, the front speeds up where energy sits; T49, ours ("seems reasonable"). All three now
  coincide with ours, so those runs test a shared expectation.
- **Other micro degrees of freedom** (her words, 16:00 ET, put in order): there are other degrees of freedom at the micro
  level, in triads, individually, or in combinations of K relationships, and they also shape X's barriers. And the piece
  that stays shut may be the separate floor on which some degrees of freedom (time, space, others) still exist at the
  micro level. *Ours, unverified:* in this model the only degrees of freedom are the links, and a barrier is a count of
  squares and surplus squares; anything more (a variable on each triad, on each point, or on groups of K links) is a new
  ingredient, which is the "other models" decision of Update 28 and needs its form written before any run (S1). A rung
  with no way downhill is exactly a separate floor in her sense: a curled direction or piece that stays small while
  others open, the model's version of hidden small dimensions (Kaluza–Klein; general knowledge, to verify).
- **More directions** (her question, 16:00 ET): do the transitions with five, six or more directions follow the pattern
  of two, three and four, and could that change the burp's transactions? Her plan: learn the pattern from 2, 3 and 4;
  if no arrangement then agrees with the observed universe, design an experiment around additional scales. *Ours:* the
  ladder's walls at five directions are exact and cheap, and are being computed (`scripts/exact_ladder_d.py`).
- **Her aim, in her words:** understanding reality is "the thing that matters most in the world" to her; "with my
  creativity and intuition and your research and communication and experiment design capabilities, I think we can make
  meaningful progress." Recorded because it is why the bar in "What success means" matters: progress here means claims
  that can fail, tested so that a physicist can check them.

Update 40 (recorded 2026-10-05; the decision is of 2026-09-25, 18:55 ET): **DECISION (the author's): gravity gets a
quantity living on the points of space, on the condition that it matches curve-first gravity.** Her words: "If it
matches curve first that's right." Why it is recorded here and out of order: it was written into the working copy that
evening as "Update 33" and never committed; the record went on from the commit before it, and the number 33 was used
the next morning for her five positions of 26 September. She confirmed on 5 October that the decision stands. The text
below is the text of that evening, with its references brought up to date. Why this page changes: the model's flat
space is gapped (every move out of it costs 32, 64 or 128 with four, six or eight links), so by the published result on
gapped media it can pass on no pull at a distance at any λ (ASSUMPTIONS O60 (a)); the exact calculation of O61 shows
that a quantity on the points that can vary smoothly would give a pull of Newton's shape if relics or energy are its
sources. This was option A of `docs/design/gravity_brief.md`, section 6.

- **What "matches curve-first" requires** (her idea as recorded on the plain-language page: space has its own drive to
  curve, matter supplies the energy that starts the curve rather than causing it, gravity belongs to space and not to
  X, and wherever Einstein's account has been measured hers gives the same numbers): the quantity lives on the points
  of space, not of X; how a dip spreads is set by space's own rule (the links between points); matter and energy set
  only where a dip starts and how deep, from their own energy, so the pull scales with energy; and the dip moves matter.
- **Under S1** this is a new knob: its form, its coupling to the wiring and to energy, and its constants are to be fixed
  in a design brief (`docs/design/gravity_field_brief.md`, not yet written; *written 2026-10-20 as `docs/design/soft_space_brief.md`, Update 76*) before any run, and every setting run is
  published. The model with it is our family, never CQG.
- *Ours, unverified (general knowledge, to verify):* in Einstein's gravity, slow objects fall almost entirely because
  time runs at different rates in different places (the weak-field metric's time part carries Newton's potential);
  the curving of space matters mainly for fast things such as light. The model has no time, so the quantity on the
  points would stand for the missing part: how fast time runs at each point. This connects with Update 25 (time as a
  fourth curled direction) and with the local pace rule she adopted the next day (Update 36, 20:00 ET: the pace of the
  present is set by local conditions, slower where matter sits). One way to make it literal, a candidate only: a
  point's value sets how often it may change, so its clock runs fast or slow, and whatever wanders collects where
  clocks run slow.
- **The author's question, the same minute:** is the missing pull just three dimensions against four (space against
  spacetime)? *Ours:* yes in that sense and not in the other. A fourth space-like direction in the network does not
  help: the eight-link model's flat space is stiffer still (its cheapest move out costs 128, against 64 with six
  links). What the six-link model lacks is time, the fourth dimension of spacetime, which is where the everyday pull
  lives in Einstein's account; the quantity on the points is the model's stand-in for it.
- **How it sits with what came after** (*ours*, 5 October). Position 2 of Update 33 (gravity and the count of hidden
  degrees of freedom) and the drive-and-cost reading of Update 35 are about *why* space would curve; this decision is
  about *what carries* a pull across a distance in a model whose flat space cannot. They do not compete. The entropic
  route (a pull from counting, at a temperature above zero) is the other candidate carrier, and it is tested first
  because it needs no new knob (the hidden count and the warm-bath pull, ASSUMPTIONS O87).

Update 41 (2026-10-05): **the author's decisions of 4 and 5 October: the first opening is dark energy's; the target is
the shares at spacetime's birth, not today's; the fair clock; and what the birth shares are once the light is counted.**
Why this page changes: two chat sessions on 4 and 5 October worked from a copy of the repository, and nothing they
decided had been written here (`docs/HANDOFF_2026-10-05_chat.md`); and her answers of 5 October to the assistant's
questions decide what later runs aim at. Details: ASSUMPTIONS O86 to O90; PREREGISTRATION T51.

- **DECISION (4 October): the first direction to open releases dark energy.** Her reason: with the other directions
  still curled, its release cannot disperse or interact as matter and radiation do, so it stays as the energy of space
  itself, and part of it pays the next push. This keeps Update 36's first step and gives it a reason. **Not decided:**
  which of the other two openings is ordinary matter and which is dark matter, and which release pays which push.
  Accepted by her: once all three are open they are alike again. *(Annotated 2026-10-19, consistency audit: set aside
  in Update 50, with the whole order-of-openings story; dark energy as the energy of space itself stands.)*
- **DECISION (4 October): the cosmological-constant problem is carried openly, not solved.** The first opening's net
  release has to be nearly zero, and the ledger's whole numbers cannot say why. The pages say so plainly.
- **DECISION (4 October): the ladder is rebuilt with a plane, two directions together, as the unit of opening**, exact
  first on the built tori, then sealed runs; it decides whether "red" is a direction or a plane. **Open, and hers to
  settle before it is built:** what a plane opening is in the model. *Ours:* two readings. (i) Bookkeeping only: the
  step from fully curled to two directions open is counted as one opening; nothing new is computed. (ii) A rule: one
  direction cannot open alone, which is a tie that charges a point for having exactly one direction open; the exact
  walls of two such shapes are in O89.
- **Time (4 October): her view stands.** Time exists because matter moves through three-dimensional space; X has order
  but not time; relativity is the evidence. "Red is time" is a reading she thinks unlikely but would test; in this model
  it can be tested only through order and cost. **No new model with relativity's time built in** for the root question.
- **Where she expects to work: λ about 1.02, six links, with the tie** (the counting drive beats the curling cost only
  within about 2 % of λ = 1, O55; the 6-cube is stuck for 1 < λ < 1.2, O41).
- **Method measurements are wanted where they tie to published models:** nucleation and growth (done, T37), the hidden
  count, the pull in a warm bath written as force = temperature × change of entropy with distance. **Held:** the causal
  order read from the opening front. *Ours:* as defined in chat (p comes before q if p opened earlier and q lies within
  the front's reach) it puts the light cone in by hand, so a causal-set estimator would return what was put in; it comes
  back if an order can be defined from the dynamics alone.
- **Paper 1.** On hold at arXiv pending a reader or a journal. Her decision of 5 October: the request to the model's
  author to be a reader, and Gate C's question, wait until after his talk. A draft is kept locally; nothing is sent
  from here.
- **DECISION (5 October): the target is the shares at spacetime's birth, not today's.** Her words: "we are hoping to
  match spacetime's birth ratios not current."
- **What the birth shares are** (*ours, unverified: settled cosmology applied by us, general knowledge, to verify with a
  physicist; it restores O18 and corrects Update 35*). Update 35 took the birth shares to be ordinary : dark matter :
  dark energy = 0.157 : 0.843 : 0. That leaves out the light. Cold matter thins out more slowly than light as space
  grows, so going back in time matter's share shrinks: at temperature T all matter together is about 0.8 eV / T of the
  energy, dark matter about 0.67 eV / T, and the mass of ordinary matter about 0.13 eV / T. By the time the light
  elements formed (about 1 MeV, the latest a hot beginning can be placed) dark matter was under a millionth of the energy
  and dark energy about 10⁻³⁵ of it; earlier, less. *(Corrected 6 October, O103 (a): the latest a hot beginning can be placed is 4 to 5 MeV by O89's own addendum, and there dark energy's share is about 10⁻³⁷; the 10⁻³⁵ is the 1 MeV figure.)* The 5.36 is real and does date from the start, but it compares two
  slivers: dark matter with the *mass* of ordinary matter, which is itself the billionth-part left when matter and
  antimatter cancelled. It is not the ratio of a dark release to an ordinary release. **So the birth shares to match
  are: the hot lump, almost everything; dark matter, a cold sliver of about 0.67 eV divided by the temperature of
  birth; dark energy, nothing measurable.** One number is unknown, the temperature of birth. *Added the same day, after the author asked whether this is published: it is. The inputs were checked at
  the source ([Planck18] for when matter and light were equal and for the two matter densities; [deSalas15] for how
  late the hot era can have begun, about 4 MeV), and the scaling between them is textbook. What is not published as
  one number is the share at birth itself, because the temperature of birth and the nature of dark matter are both
  unknown (O89, addendum). The application to three releases remains ours.*
- **What that changes** (*ours*). (1) Fitting the tie's two constants to 5.36 (O74, T45, and the chat's
  f = (0, a, 1.53a, 0)) aimed at a ratio the releases do not have to hit. Those runs stand as run; their target is
  withdrawn. (2) The chat's verdict that the scrap is not the dark matter "by a factor of ten" is withdrawn (O88): at
  birth 8 % is too much scrap, not too little, and what a leftover dark matter needs is that nearly all of it heals,
  which is what slower cooling does (T25). So Update 16's reading, dark matter as the leftover, is open again beside
  Update 30's, dark matter as a direction's release. Which she holds is hers; T51 measures what the first one needs.
  (3) With dark energy first and netting nothing, the shapes that match birth are those in which **one opening carries
  essentially the whole release**. There are two, with no constant to fit: nothing is released until the last direction
  opens (the three go together, her position of Update 22), or the second opening releases everything and the last
  direction is free to stay curled (her prediction for T48, and a hidden small direction). Exact walls for both are in
  O89. Neither is adopted.
- **DECISION (5 October): the fair clock for new kinetic runs.** Time is counted so that every local pair of links is
  offered equally often at every size (O76's proposal; O90). Verdicts already scored stay as scored.
- **The scrap-share reading stays out of paper 2** (5 October, on the assistant's advice; she expects the paper to
  change a good deal before it is shared).

Update 42 (2026-10-05, committed 10:49 ET): **ideas of the author's on what X is inside a black hole, what dark energy is, and how
the papers should be built.** Her words, put in order; she called them spitballing, and they are recorded as ideas to
test, not decisions, except where marked. Why this page changes: three of them say what the next exact tables and runs
should be about. Details: ASSUMPTIONS O100; `docs/papers/series_plan.md`, last section.

- **X as spaghetti.** Inside a black hole X may be two directions curled and one open, not a point: the black hole
  only has to curl two directions before the space snaps back open. That state is unstable, like X as a singularity,
  but being long in one direction it allows several seeds. Because the compression is not stable, especially with the
  tie between directions, it would not need more matter and energy from the hole for each burp; it could burp at once.
  Her questions: would X be stable in this phase, or constantly burping, or something else? *Ours, exact (O100):* in
  the toy this state exists and has been run. Untied, it is stuck for now while the curling cost is small and cannot
  hold at all once the cost passes a threshold (λ about 1.45): which of "stable for now" and "burps at once" it is, is
  a matter of one number. Given a push it has so far opened one of its two curled directions and stopped (T30, T48),
  and at the setting nearest the threshold it opened both in 5 runs of 16.
- **She asks for a table of every state of the directions**, since many arrangements can be tested. *Ours:* the exact
  ladder exists for two, three and four directions (`docs/public/curling_ladder.html`; `scripts/exact_ladder_d.py`) and
  is extended with what each state has been seen to do (O100).
- **Why the first opening nets almost nothing: perhaps because it is already open.** If X begins with one direction
  open, that direction never had to open, and dark energy would be some kind of scrap or relic of it. *Ours:* this
  gives the zero for free, which the ledger could not. It then leaves the small amount that is observed to be
  explained, and by the rule taken from [PWS09] a scrap of fixed number thins out as matter does, so a dark energy made
  of scrap needs the scrap's number to grow with the space. Not decided.
- **Time.** "X has order but not time" holds either way: with one direction open nothing moves, so there is no time.
- **Matter and antimatter.** She asks whether the energies released cancel somehow, leaving only the net one to 5.36
  of the two kinds of matter. *Ours, from abstracts read on 5 October ([KLZ09], [PV13], [MRM12]):* in standard physics
  the hot lump held almost equal amounts of matter and antimatter, which turned to light as it cooled, and ordinary
  matter is the small excess left over. A published line of work, asymmetric dark matter, holds that dark matter is
  the same kind of leftover excess, which would make 5.36 a ratio of two leftovers, as she guesses, and not of two
  releases. And the standard way to make such an excess needs the universe out of balance for a moment, which a sharp
  change with a moving front supplies: in the best-studied proposal it is made at the walls of the bubbles of a
  first-order transition. So the burp's front is where an excess could be made. Nothing in the toy has anything that
  comes in opposite kinds, so this is a direction, not a result.
- **Seeds.** Would each seed be its own new spacetime, or do seeds close together join into one? Perhaps the open
  direction makes X unstable, the other two open of themselves, and the scrap is the minuscule dark energy while the
  releases are the two matters; or only one of the two releases nets energy and makes every kind of matter. *Ours, a
  first look (O100):* in the toy, seeds in one connected X usually join into one space, and in a minority of long
  tubes the opening ended as two separate spaces. "Only one opening nets energy" is one of the two shapes O89 found to
  match the make-up at birth.
- **A second line of work: the loop and the sizes of infinities.** We are probably circling the largest, most likely
  observed infinity, so a theory of maximizing, as in economics, may apply to the sizes of infinities: a loop that
  cannot be created is not a large infinity beside a strange loop that holds every configuration. She asks what
  research exists. A reading is under way (the measure problem; maximizing principles in cosmology; criteria for
  comparing infinite streams in economics).
- **DECISION: many short papers, not one long one.** "I'd rather have 15 seven-page papers, brought together as the
  full theory by a 16th, than one 90-page paper." Each short paper may take up, replicate or extend the model or the
  open question of one researcher's most recent work, as paper 1 does for Kelly, Trugenberger and Biancalana. The list
  is in `docs/papers/series_plan.md`.

Update 43 (2026-10-05, 11:40 ET by the clock): **the author's position on the measure: both the yield of a turn and
the multiplying count, and the reward is observers who evolved.** Why this page changes: the reading of O101 named a
trap in counting per turn (if one turn makes many black holes, the rate of multiplying decides what dominates) and a
gap (the yield of a turn has to be finite), and her answer says what is being counted. Her words, put in order.

- **Maximize both.** A reality with far more black holes may hold them too close together, cutting short the time in
  which molecules rearrange into life that grows in complexity, or the number of stars with planets at a temperature
  where complex molecules are stable. Those are real requirements of our reality, and they compete with simply making
  the most black holes, even if that is on paper the larger infinity. So the multiplying does not win by itself.
- **The reward is an observer**, and things that go with observers evolving serve as well: a long cold period, complex
  molecules, stars with planets at the right temperature.
- **Brains that assemble by chance in space are not real observers: they cannot evolve.** A reality has stable rules of
  order; it is not random arrangements of matter at every level of complexity. Or at least that is so for observers
  who loop as we do, which is the reality we are in.
- *Ours.* (1) This answers the trap in her terms: what is counted is observers who evolved, per turn, times how the
  turns multiply, and a setting that makes many black holes and no one to observe scores nothing. Written out, a loop's
  score is its observers per turn multiplied by a factor that grows with each generation, so over many generations
  the multiplying still dominates unless it is bounded or the two are tied together, as she argues they are: more
  black holes, sooner, means less cold time. That trade is the thing to make exact. (2) Counting something that goes
  with observers, not observers themselves, is what the one published maximizing principle does: it counts the entropy
  produced, which is dominated by starlight warming dust ([BHKP07], abstract only). (3) Requiring that observers have
  evolved is one of the published answers to the chance-brain problem ([Freivogel11] reviews it). (4) The gap of O101
  remains: a turn's yield has to be finite, and her list (a long cold period, stars with planets) is finite in any one
  turn once it is counted up to the last star. *(Annotated 2026-10-19, consistency audit: "per turn, times how the turns
  multiply" is superseded by Updates 67, 68 and 71: per universe within a setting, by its own age, once per civilization.)*

Update 44 (2026-10-06, 03:56 ET by the clock): **three AI-generated reviews of the programme, checked against the
record, and the author's decisions on them.** Why this page changes: the reviews put four decisions to the author and
she made them; one idea of hers is recorded here for the first time; and from here a failed prediction is written in a
fixed form. What the reviews got right and wrong is ASSUMPTIONS O103; they are AI output, and their physics is ours,
unverified, like any other. Two of them call the hypothesis "Omega", its name in an earlier version of the theory; it
is X.

- **DECISION: the framing stays.** The reviews proposed freezing a dated "version 1" of the hypothesis with three to
  five predictions and scoring it, so that the hypothesis could not absorb every failure. Her answer: "I do not want
  to change this. The hypothesis I can read before I start every work session to ground me in where we are at big
  picture." The pages keep "first version didn't hold · in progress", and the claim at the top of the programme
  draft stays current.
- **DECISION: each piece is to be killed; the hypothesis is adjusted.** On "optimize for making it easy to kill": "I
  agree with this in theory, let's see how it goes. The thing is big picture, I will adjust but not kill the
  hypothesis. Killing it is giving up on having a clear picture of how reality works, that's our real goal. But
  definitely want to treat each tiny piece as something to kill." Written into CLAUDE.md at the head of rules 10 to 13.
- **DECISION: four working rules adopted** (CLAUDE.md rules 10 to 13): four kinds of statement, never silently promoted
  ("this seems good"); decisive tests before patches, the four questions answered before any new mechanism is proposed
  ("seems reasonable"); a red-team pass at each milestone; outside theories as targets and a close number not a result
  until checked. Rule 11's form for a failed prediction, "previous claim → failed because → replacement → new
  falsification test", applies from this update on; its first use is below. *(Annotated 2026-10-19, consistency audit:
  Update 55 shortened rule 11's four answers to one question before a replacement is promoted; the one-line form stays
  on the programme page; CLAUDE.md rule 11 is the current text.)*
- **The author's conjecture, recorded for the first time** (hers; "my best guess with current information"). Time as
  we experience it exists because matter moves through open space (Update 41). Over a loop's turn, matter and energy
  gather into black holes; when all of it has, nothing moves through space, and so there is no time: the turn ends.
  In the form the reviews ask for: *time is an outcome of relational change in the open phase; a late or re-curled
  state might cease to support the processes that function as clocks; whether a turn reaches such a state, and
  whether re-curling inside black holes supplies the mechanism, remain to be derived.* Not an established mechanism;
  and not the artificial cutoff of eternal-inflation measures ([Freivogel11]), which is a regulator for assigning
  probabilities and a different thing. It supplies the "stated end" that O101 said a turn needs. The toy has no time
  and cannot test it. *(Annotated 2026-10-19, consistency audit: reworded in Update 59: each universe ends when its last
  black hole has dissolved, and the whole-universe reset is no longer required.)*
- **Rule 11's first use, on piece 5.** Previous claim (the author's, Update 22): one activation opens all three curled
  directions and releases the whole burp. Failed because: in this family each direction has its own wall and the
  energy ties nothing; a push the size of the cheapest move opens one direction, or one cube, and stops (T33, T40, T44
  to T49; O103 (i)). Replacement (hers, Updates 39 and 42): X is one connected piece with a direction already open,
  and a bath supplies each push in turn (T53, running; *read 2026-10-09: ADVANCES ONLY at every length and both λ, the
  second direction opening only in a minority before the space is damaged; Update 46*). New falsification test: the exact barrier to the next opening
  of a connected one-open X (T54, a draft): if it rises with the opened patch without bound at every λ at which X is
  stuck, untied and under the tie, no finite push opens all and that branch is abandoned in this family.
- **Open to her, not decided.** (a) Claim 4's word "first order". The reviews are right that the tube's change is the
  decay of a stable-for-now state, not a first-order transition in the thermodynamic sense; paper 1 says so under
  "Not claimed", S2′ says "a decay with an activation cost", and the README and the public pages now carry the clause.
  The claim's own text is hers; the proposed wording is "sharp, with a bounded lump released: a decay with an
  activation cost". *(Annotated 2026-10-19, consistency audit: (a) decided in Update 56; claim 4 now says "a jump in
  energy".)* (b) Whether piece 10 stays attached to the toy's front measurements, which do not bear on time
  dilation and read the opposite way (O93), or is kept as her philosophy until a model with time exists. (c) Dark
  energy as a release (Update 41) or as energy the final state keeps, the curling cost of a direction that stays
  curled (O103 (e)). (d) Her prediction for T51, still owed, and for T54 before it runs. (e) Whether paper 1 is revised
  now or after a physicist has read it (candidates in `docs/papers/curled_torus/v2_changes.md`). (f) The reference for
  the Fife paper the third review cites, so that it can be read rather than cited from a review.
- **A correction to Update 41:** "about 1 MeV, the latest a hot beginning can be placed" is 4 to 5 MeV by O89's own

Update 45 (2026-10-08, 05:51 ET by the clock): **DECISION (the author's, "Yes proceed"): three working methods
adopted from an outside project.** Why this page changes: the author read a public account of an AI-assisted search
for a planet in telescope data, in which the agents did the labour, the person chose the questions and the pass/fail
rules, fresh read-only sessions and a second model reviewed the work, a whole year of data was held back and predicted
from the other two, and 36 catalogs were searched before the signal was called unreported. She asked which of its
methods this programme lacks. Most it already has (pre-registration, rules 10 to 13, corrections kept visible, the
discreteness check on two-hump histograms). Three it did not, and she adopted them:

- **The red-team pass is done by a fresh, read-only reviewer** (CLAUDE.md rule 12, second half): no history from the
  work, read-only access, a different model where one is available, given the repository and the question but never the
  conclusions.
- **A held-out size for every size claim** (rule 14): fit on the smaller sizes, write a number with a range for the
  largest, score the size verdict there only. This is the programme's version of the held-back year. It is weaker than
  the original, and said so: there the hidden year was real data the analysis could not touch; here the held-out size
  is another run of the same model.
- **A written search behind every "we have not found"** (rule 15), on a template in `docs/reading/notes/`.

Not adopted, with the reason: proofs checked by a proof assistant (Lean), as in recent published AI mathematics. The
exact results here are already checked by brute force in the tests, which covers most of the same risk at far lower
cost. It comes back if an exact claim is ever too large to enumerate.

*Ours, exact:* paper 1 already contains one sentence that rule 15 now covers ("A different kind of change has not, as
far as we have found, been studied in this model"); its search note is owed before any revision of paper 1 is posted
(TASKS, the reviews of 6 October, item 9).

  any other way, curls a macro direction of the space itself: RE-CURLS at the largest energies.
- **What is exact under her tie** (O106): every rung of the ladder sits 3a per point above flat space, so under "all at
  the last" curling one direction costs the same as curling all three, 3a per point; the first fold pays the whole
  price and the later folds are free. And under the table as implemented a damaged point (more open directions than
  three) costs no tie, so the tie charges curling and not breaking.

  into one point or spread through a bath) under the tie and untied, at λ = 1.10 and 1.25, at energies up to twice

  PREREGISTRATION T57 before any run; ours is MELTS or HEALS at every energy, tied and untied, with no direction

  cheaper than curling.

Update 52 (2026-10-10, 16:25 ET by the clock): **the author's lean on the three shares: the burp is the hot lump, the
scrap is the best guess for the dark matter, and dark energy is what does not thin out.** Why this page changes:
Update 50 left dark matter open "between the scrap and something else" and dark energy's source open; asked to bring
the programme page's piece 6 up to date, the author gave the direction it should lean. Her words: "adjust this
section so it leans toward our current view: the one curled direction and the scrap best guess of what it is given
currnet evidence. doesnt expand = dark energy, or was it dark matter.... remind me?"

- **Recorded as her lean, not a decision; the mapping below is the assistant's reading of her words and is hers to
  correct.** The burp, released when the slab's one curled direction opens (Updates 47, 50), is the hot lump: light and
  ordinary matter. The scrap is the best current guess for the dark matter (Update 16's reading, open again since
  Update 41). Dark energy is the energy that space itself keeps.
- **The reminder she asked for** (*ours; general knowledge, to verify with a physicist, as O89*). What tells the three
  apart is how each thins out as space grows. A fixed number of things in more room thins out: ordinary matter, dark
  matter, and a fixed number of knots would. Light thins faster. Dark energy is the one that does not thin: the same
  density however large space gets. So "does not thin as space expands" is dark energy.
- **What the toy has for each** (*exact and measured; the toy has no expansion, so none of this is a measurement of
  thinning*). Under the tie "all at the last" one opening carries the whole release (O89, O106). The knots have a
  fixed energy each and a number set at the opening (O104, O107); the stubborn ones hold about one per cent of the
  release, where birth can afford far less than a millionth, so the guess rests on the seed count. Fully open space
  keeps exactly nothing (flat space is the floor, Q18); the only thing in the toy that would not thin is the cost of
  a direction that stays curled, which is far too large (O103 (e)).
- **Also hers, the same day, about the programme page.** A prediction about a thing that turned out not to be real
  (T58's second curl, for long waits that were mostly the detector's) is not discussed on that page unless real
  evidence appears of an unknown mechanism adding to the tail; the registration keeps it as written. And the
  sentence that the knots are not seams is dropped from the page as obvious by now; T55's verdict stays in the
  record.
- **Nothing here is a new knob or a run.** The claim at the head of the programme page, confirmed by her on 9
  October, still says the two identifications are open; this lean sits in piece 6 under her name until she confirms
  or corrects it.

  whole direction, flat space ends half broken and not curled at all: 582 of 640 replicas MELTED, 58 HEALED, none

  end states saved by the four maps of paper 1, 9,208 are the same tube with its seam shifted by two: six steps
  around where the tube was four, the least a flat torus can be, at every size from 64 to 192 points. So in these

Update 55 (2026-10-11): **DECISION (the author's, "adopt"): two lanes, three rules changed, three added.** Why this
page changes: outside feedback on the process, which she passed on, said that the rules protect claims well and tax
looking: after a failed prediction a new idea had to wait for four written answers (rule 11), new ideas went to a
parked list by default (rule 9), every sentence on every page had to say its kind (rule 10), and each failure added
a rule while nothing ever removed one. Checked against the record, the diagnosis holds, with two corrections: no
idea of hers actually waited on rule 11 or was parked by rule 9 in recent weeks, and the heavier costs were each
result being written in seven to nine places and clock times kept to the minute. Her word on the proposal: "adopt".

- **Kept as they were**, by the feedback's list and hers: reproduce before extending, pre-registration, append-only
  results, the assumption register, "a close number is not a result", held-out sizes, prior-work notes.
- **Changed and added.** The rule text is in `CLAUDE.md` (rules 9, 10, 11 and 16 to 18) and is not repeated here: a
  sandbox for looking, which can kill a claim but never support one, never opens unread registered results, and
  promotes only onto fresh data; ideas start in a notebook and are tried at once; kinds of statement are labeled on
  what outside readers see; one question, not four, before a replacement is promoted; one fact, one home; a review
  of the rules each month, the first in `docs/RULES_REVIEW.md`.
- **What does not change.** S1 to S5 and the claims. A new knob still needs her dated decision here. Nothing already
  scored is rescored.
- **From this update on** this page records decisions only. Her ideas go to `sandbox/NOTEBOOK.md` in her words.

  establish a first-order transition: "So I don't care whether it's first order. I'm just interested in if there is
  a jump in energy at some sizes we are measuring." And then: "I want claim 4's 'first order' reworded to match."
  Claim 4's last sentence now reads "It came with a jump in energy: it released a bounded lump of energy (Δρ) that
  became matter and radiation." The old words are struck through in place. **What follows.** S2 (two humps, and
  the valley deepening with size) stays as the bar for the published question and is no longer a bar the
  hypothesis sets for itself; T6's verdicts stand as scored. The hypothesis's own route is scored by S2′, as since
  Update 13. **What it costs, said plainly:** "a jump at the sizes measured" says nothing about an endlessly large
  system, which is what "first order" was about. A physicist will ask (S5), and the answer is what paper 1 already
  says: a stable-for-now state gives way and releases a fixed amount per point, as far as it has been run.
- **DECISION: tests with a price on loops of six.** Her words: "Yes let's do some tests where there is a price on
  loops of 6", after writing "if this kept happening" beside the reviewer's sentence that a second large direction
  has not appeared in the end states. **The form, ours, fixed here before any registered run:** a new term
  μ × the sum over links of (loops of six through the link − 6), counted only where positive. It is the toy's own
  rule one loop-length up: in a wide flat sheet every link lies on exactly six loops of six and pays nothing, and
  on a tube six around a link lies on more. One constant, μ ≥ 0. Every value run is published. With it the model is
  our family, never the published model. Sandbox looks so far are in `sandbox/LOG.md` and are not evidence. A
  registered test needs a tested kernel (rule 6), a held-out size (rule 14) and her prediction.

Update 57 (2026-10-15): **three answers of the author's to the programme page's open items: piece 9 adopts the

  becomes a bar the loop has to meet and not evidence that it does; the object the amplitudes live on is open, since

  Everett's branches, which is untested (O125). None of these is a reason not to adopt it as the working rule; each is
  what paper 13 and piece 13's toy now have to say. The quantum rungs already scored (T15 rungs 0, 0b, 3a) stand.

  that may only curl, reverses Update 32's "one kind of energy pays every push": "Correct. that was based off the idea

  curling energy is a thing". Under S1 this is a new ingredient and a new kind of state. What is decided is that it

  fixed in a design brief before any registered run, from her answers to the ten questions in the notebook, and every
  setting run is published. Sandbox looks may run first and may narrow it, never support it (rule 16). What stands of
  Update 32: in X the directions are alike, and chance picks which opens first. The untied verdicts stand as scored.
- **Pieces 13 and 14: "tbd".** Not decided. They stay on the programme page as drafts marked ours.
- *Ours:* the knots' prediction (whether the stubborn knots are still stuck at a curling cost of zero, piece 7) is
  still owed; her message broke off before it.

Update 58 (2026-10-16): **DECISION (the author's, "I think it's time to remove the tie then"): the direction tie
"all at the last" is withdrawn; the hypothesis's three-direction runs are untied from here.** Why this page changes:
the tie was a knob decided under S1 (Update 47) and its withdrawal is a decision of the same kind. Her reasoning, from

to curvature plus a curling cost, 4(λ − 1) per curled link (Update 23), with every direction costing the same and
carrying its own wall. *(Annotated 2026-10-19, consistency audit: the energy stays this, but the layer-form toy has more
ingredients than it: curling-energy stores (Update 60), the clean two-switch move and pooled payment (Update 62),
compaction with a varying count (Updates 63, 65), the daughter ledger (Update 66) and the gate g1 (Update 69).)*

- **What stands.** X is the slab, two directions open and one curled (Update 47's shape statement, now without its

- **Verdicts.** Every tied verdict stands as scored (T44 to T46 with their ties; T54 tied; T56; T57 tied). T65,

- **Rule 11, on piece 5, fourth use.** Previous claim (hers, Update 47): under "all at the last" the slab opens into

  Untied, the directions do curl separately again (each has its own wall; O55). Whether curling energy stays as an
  ingredient, and whether Update 32's "one kind of energy pays every push" returns with it, is hers to say; nothing is

- **Her answer, the same morning: "Yes curling energy. Yes curl separately. Now I understand better ofc they curl
  separately. I just couldn't picture the stable slab yet."** So curling energy stays as an ingredient (Update 57), now

Update 59 (2026-10-16, evening): **the author's handoff of 16 October, recorded: the black hole is the parent's
own geometry re-curled, not a slab contained inside it; what infall raises is g, not λ; nucleation is a rising
chance, not a threshold; and the whole-universe reset is no longer required, so a daughter space may form while the
black hole remains in the parent.** Why this page changes: two of these are decisions about the hypothesis's shape,
and S1 and rule 9 require that what is decided be written here and what is only an idea stay in the notebook. The
document is hers, drafted with another assistant and passed on; it is kept as received in
`docs/theory/relational_cosmology_handoff_2026-10-16.md`, her words from it are in `sandbox/NOTEBOOK.md`, and it is
read against the record in `docs/theory/relational_cosmology_review_2026-10-16.md` (short form: ASSUMPTIONS O131).
It calls the hypothesis "Omega"; this page writes X. Its research items T1 to T8 are H1 to H8 in the record, since
T1 to T8 are registrations here. Merging this update is her acceptance of the wording.

- **DECISION: the black hole's inside is the parent's own geometry curled.** Her words: the two extended directions
  of the slab "are those of the parent universe itself. They are not severed at the event horizon. Instead, existing
  spatial relationships curl inward in a whirlpool-like configuration. This is not a proposal involving a little,
  separately contained slab inside a black hole." This revises the knot-and-shell picture of the same morning
  (notebook: a 64-point knot with a 4 × L × L shell inside the horizon), which stays in the notebook as an idea.
  *Ours:* in the toy this is the fold of piece 11, where nothing has yet curled from energy alone (T21, T26, T27,
  T34, T42, T57); the reading changes what the fold is of, not what it waits on (a carrier for a pull, piece 8).
  **Confirmed by the author, 2026-10-16, later the same evening ("correct"). This decision is hers as worded.**
- **DECISION: the reset is no longer required.** Her words: "The black hole might remain present in the parent
  universe while the daughter has its own emergent spacetime. ... The earlier whole-universe-reset model may still be
  investigated, but it is no longer a requirement for daughter-universe formation." The loop (Updates 23, 26, 33,
  43) stays as a candidate beside the daughter picture. *Ours:* the per-turn measure of Update 23 needs the reset; a
  daughter that opens inside a parent that goes on is counted per generation, which is the branching case O101
  flagged. Which count she holds is hers (piece 12).
  **Confirmed by the author, 2026-10-16, later the same evening ("correct"), with the count decided.** Her words,
  put in order: each individual universe is reset only when its last black hole has dissolved; that is the end state
  each is working toward; stuff keeps unfolding; every black hole births multiple new ones; that is what we can call
  a larger infinity: the length of time just as long, but each universe producing a trillion more rather than just
  one more; and that makes this more likely from the anthropic observer's perspective, which is a nice cohesion.
  *What is decided:* piece 12's count is the branching one. *(Annotated 2026-10-19, consistency audit: refined by
  Updates 67, 68 and 71: within a setting universes are counted per universe, each whole and by its own age, once per
  civilization, so the branching adds copies and no weight; a count of the tree at one moment has no meaning (Update
  68). The measure as now defined is in `docs/design/loop_measure_brief.md`, sections 4 and 7.)* A universe's turn still ends (her conjecture of 6
  October, Update 44, now worded as its last black hole dissolving), but the next generation is not one reset of
  the whole: it is every black hole's daughter, so the tree multiplies by a large factor each generation at the
  same cold time per universe. *Ours:* this is the branching case O101 named, now chosen rather than avoided. With
  the cold time held equal, the branching factor is what the selection acts on, which is Smolin's selection for the
  most black holes with her observer requirement as the filter; the "larger infinity" is a faster-growing tree, not
  a larger cardinality (the count of universes is countable either way), and that is what the anthropic weight
  compares. The trade she named on 5 October (more black holes sooner cuts the cold time) is the constraint that
  keeps the branching from running away, and it is the function piece 12 still has to write down.
- **Standing positions, restated by her.** λ is a universal constant, the same in parent and daughter, not raised
  by infall (Update 23, extended across generations). What infall raises is g, the excitation; the chance of an
  opening rises with it and there is no single threshold. *(Annotated 2026-10-19: "no single threshold" replaced by
  Update 69's gate, her answer in Update 72; the rising chance can stand as how the threshold looks from outside.)*
  *Exact, from the toy's machinery:* in a sealed box g is
  read off the stores (Q12; O108), so a feed that raises the stores' energy raises g by construction; H2 of her list
  needs no derivation. Points are not fundamental, so a daughter need not be smaller than its parent: her answer
  to her own question of the morning (how the parent holds more than 640 points). *Ours:* the toy's count is fixed
  and a varying count is claim 3's parked decision; the toy cannot represent this and does not contradict it.
- **Her research priority, adopted as the order of work:** "Establish the energy-coupled nucleation and stable
  propagation mechanism before attempting to demonstrate the full cosmological interpretation." That is the
  registered feed run already named by rule 11's fifth use on piece 5 (PREREGISTRATION T65), with the three
  outcomes of her H3 (first escape, a front that persists, completion) recorded separately. H5 to H7 wait on it.
- **Two cautions carried from the review.** Her waiting-time law is the four-link tube's (paper 1), measured to
  hold with nothing fitted there; it does not govern the slab, whose growing patch is a completed-patch energy of
  about 225 at λ = 1.25 (T54, O118) and which no g has opened cleanly in a bath, from a spark or under a feed (T53,
  T56, T65, the feed look). And "melts into disorder" is withdrawn by O123; with too little room the product is
  damaged, not melted.

Update 132 (2026-10-27, 14:45 UTC by the clock; written by Claude Fable 5.1 without the author): **the fifth independent
audit by a different model (Codex), of the record of 26 to 27 October: the four-switch count was not complete, the row
segment breaks the hard rule, and three readings were overstated. Corrected in place (marked "O163").** Brief
`docs/reviews/2026-10-27_codex_audit_brief.md`, pinned to 9f43c2a4; report `docs/reviews/2026-10-27_codex_report.md`; the
record is ASSUMPTIONS O163.

  cells are SEED-LINKED by the declared rule. (6) O159's "nothing it reported was found wrong" is withdrawn: the fourth

- **What stands.** Both held-out readings (T71 AS PREDICTED, 152 of 152; T74 HOLDS AT L = 16, 18 of 18, every secondary

- **What it makes plain** (*ours*). A prune copied from a shallower search is the kind of defect a complete count hides
  until a fresh reader replays a discarded sequence; and a look that checks its starting state against her rule must

Update 133 (2026-10-27, 16:42 UTC by the clock): **DECISION (the author's): the hard rule is softened; and her picture of
the twin X, with each curled direction carrying its own curling cost.** Her words are in `sandbox/NOTEBOOK.md` under this
time, verbatim. Why this page changes: Q1 (Update 128, O159, O163) asked whether to keep her hard rule (squares held at
exactly 3N), extend the moves, or soften it; under the hard rule no count-keeping move out of clean space has been found
at up to three switches, and the four-switch count is open again (O163). She chose to soften. Her description answers Q2
in her terms and restates Q3.

- **DECISION: the soft form.** "proceed w soften": the hard rule becomes a penalty, H_soft = H + μ (S - 3N)^2, so the
  square count may drift from 3N at a price that grows with the drift. Under S1 this is a new term; μ is scanned, never
  tuned, and every value run is published. Still to fix before any run (program item 10 of
  `docs/design/next_experiments_2026-10-26.md`): whether μ is held fixed or scales as μ0/N (one, fixed in advance; the
  scan must not choose afterward), her prediction, her clock word, and the starting X. The row segment between two flaws
  (her idea of 20 October) is admitted by the soft form at a fixed price, 4 μ in two directions and 144 μ for the
  six-link strip, with lengthening free (*exact arithmetic* from O163's deficits): a seed paid once that then grows for
  nothing, to be watched for in the soft runs, not assumed.
- **Her picture (hers).** The rod sits at the center of a shell; one feed line runs from the center to the shell's north
  pole and one to its south pole; the handedness of matter and antimatter twists is concentrated at one pole or the other.
  When the rod on either side reaches capacity it opens a new space that carries the whole barrier and brings its surplus
  of right- or left-handed twists; as soon as the first side goes, so does the other, making the opposite daughter. Both
  open from the same rod, in a shell of two degrees of freedom with two whirlpool poles and the one-degree-of-freedom rod
  like a collar between them; the two twins carry the halves that become a surplus in each. Each curled direction carries
  its own curling cost, 4(λ - 1) on each side, with the shell's polarization represented in the difference between
  λ and one.
- **What is exact about it** (*ours, checked on flat, slab and rod at N = 512*). (1) Each curled direction carrying its
  own cost is the untied ladder (O41, O49): slab a, rod 2a per point, a = 4(λ - 1); each opening releases exactly a
  per point, so "half each" holds with nothing chosen by us. This replaces the pair tie f = (0, 0, a, 0) of Q3, which did
  not give half each (O159 (h)). (2) The toy's energy is H = 4 uncurl + 4(λ - 1) curl and S - 3N = (curl - uncurl)/4.
  So her 4(λ - 1) a side is exactly what the toy charges for curl that nothing balances. Under the soft penalty an
  unbalanced rod also pays μ N^2/4 and a slab μ N^2/16, so the first opening releases aN + 3 μ N^2/16 and the second
  aN + μ N^2/16: the halves are unequal by μ N^2/8 (with μ = μ0/N, by μ0 N/8, a fixed amount per point). (3) If the
  shell carries anticurl that balances the rod's curl, the penalty vanishes, but in the toy each unit of anticurl costs 4,
  so a side costs 4 λ per unit of curl, not 4(λ - 1). Her formula and a balanced X agree only if the shell's
  anticurl is priced at nothing, which is a new term and hers to decide (S1); "the polarization in λ - 1" may be
  exactly that, and is asked.
- **What it is up against** (*measured, on the rule-breaking rod, no soft penalty*). Untied, the first direction opens
  and the second does not follow: NEVER OPENS (T30, O64), ADVANCES ONLY (T48 at 1.25, O91; T53, O105). "As soon as the
  first side goes, so does the other" has not been seen in this family without a tie; under the soft form it is untested.
- **What the toy cannot hold yet.** Signed twist (Update 97's label is declared, not built), so the matter/antimatter
  surplus at the poles and the opposite daughters cannot be tested; a shell with two poles has not been built in the
  toy, whose spaces are tori. *Published relative (general knowledge, to verify):* a flow on a sphere must have
  whirlpools whose indices add to 2 (Poincare-Hopf, the hairy-ball theorem), so two whirlpools at the poles is the
  simplest such flow; the twin universes of opposite kind are [BFT18] (unread).
- **What runs next.** The soft term in the six-link kernel with rule-6 tests (networkx brute force, incremental
  exactness, constraint preservation, same seed); then program item 10 is pre-registered with her prediction once μ's
  scaling and the starting X are fixed. Nothing is run before that.
- **Her answers, 17:41 UTC the same day** (verbatim in the notebook). (A1) **μ = μ₀/N**: the penalty is a fixed amount per
  point, like every other energy in the toy. (A2) **Anticurl costs λ, as curling does**, "would be 1 except for the
  handedness concentrating closer to poles": each unit of anticurl costs 4λ in place of the toy's 4, so H_A = 4λ·uncurl +
  4(λ − 1)·curl, the toy's own energy at λ = 1. *Exact (arithmetic; checked to 10⁻¹³ on curled and melted six-link
  states):* H_A = λ·H(λ′) with λ′ = 2 − 1/λ. So her pricing at (λ, g) is the existing toy at λ′ and coupling g/λ, and the
  soft penalty becomes μ/λ: no new kernel, and every result already on the λ line is a result of her energy at λ =
  1/(2 − λ′) (the tube's window 1 < λ′ < 1.5 is her 1 < λ < 2; T7's λ′ = 1.25 is her λ = 4/3). A balanced X, curl and
  anticurl equal, costs (8λ − 4) per unit of curl under it. Asked: one λ everywhere, or λ above 1 only near the poles?
  (A3) The shell holds curl and anticurl; matter becomes curl where it has no room to move, more curl (matter) toward the
  north pole and more anticurl (antimatter) toward the south. *In the toy (ours):* curling a region makes curl and melting
  one makes anticurl (melted six-link states carry uncurl and no curl), so the nearest buildable X is the rod with a
  curled cap on one side and a melted cap on the other, balanced so S = 3N; the toy has no handedness, so matter and
  antimatter are not in it. (A4) **Her prediction: the soft runs make the space open with the twin daughters.**

Update 134 (2026-10-27, 19:00 UTC by the clock): **the author's direction: toward the twist rules, with spacetime
emerging from twist and curl.** Her words are in `sandbox/NOTEBOOK.md` under this time, verbatim. Why this page
changes: two sandbox looks of the same afternoon (LOG) found that her soft rule couples the curled north and the
anticurled south of her X, but that the south's anticurl, made by melting, heals by itself and the north then breaks
rather than opens. She asked whether curl and anticurl could be mirror images, as a signed twist is, rather than curl
and melt (*exact:* in the toy they are not; a link's curl runs from +1 to −4, the negative side mostly reached by
disorder, and under her pricing the two sides cost differently). Of the two routes offered she chose the twist.

- **Her direction.** Spacetime should emerge from two degrees of freedom, twist and curl (her H1 of 25 October). Twist
  makes curling more expensive, because it can polarize handedness; so much twist means curl is harder, the surplus is
  passed to the daughter universes, and the loop goes on, a new pair of universes for every black hole, which we
  experience as just our universe. Every combination of twist and curl exists; where many are packed together they form
  the first daughter pair; from inside that two-dimensional space we experience a three-dimensional world, a strange
  loop like a video camera recording the screen it projects onto, complex and emergent as a video player's design.
- **Her rules, as candidate forms for her to confirm** (questions T1 to T4 in `docs/design/owner_questions_2026-10-24.md`;
  *ours*, the translation): (1) twist raises the curling cost by an amount set by the twist's size, not its sign; (2) a
  whole twist with half a unit of curling energy is one whole charge; (3) a curl forms at the opposite angle from its
  anticurl; (4) curl spreads at one link per tick, slower near twist. The twist is the phase of the light field already
  built (`graphity.u1`, Update 96): its twist round each square runs from −π to π and costs the same either way, so plus
  and minus are mirror images by construction.
- **What is put in and what could be found** (*ours*). Rule (1) makes "lots of twist means curl is harder" true by
  construction; it is not a result. What a run could show is what follows: whether the release then goes where she says,
  whether a front slows near twist (her rule (4) and Update 36's pace rule predict it), and whether a curl and its
  anticurl form at opposite angles without that being imposed.
- **Published relatives** (general knowledge, none read by us, to verify): a 3D world experienced from a 2D one is the
  holographic principle ('t Hooft; Susskind), and the published model's own programme rests on a 2D holographic screen
  ([T23], [T24], read); the camera filming its own screen is Hofstadter's image of a strange loop; "every combination
  exists" is Tegmark's mathematical universe; a pair of universes from every black hole is near Smolin's cosmological
  natural selection and [BFT18].
- **Before anything is built:** her answers T1 to T4, and question 5 (how a phase rides a switch). The light field runs
  on a fixed graph today; riding it on switching wiring is new code with rule-6 tests. Nothing is run before that.
- **DECISION (the author's, 20:04 UTC the same day): curl and anticurl cost the same, 4(λ − 1) a unit**, a pair
  always made together at twice that; "λ stops shaping anything ... this is fine". This replaces her pricing of 17:41
  UTC (Update 133, A2). *Exact:* the toy's energy becomes (λ − 1)·H(2), the existing toy at λ′ = 2 with λ − 1 as the
  energy unit; at λ′ = 2 no curled X of the toy is stuck for now (O13, O41, O49). So **what holds X, if anything, must
  come from the twist rules** (twist raising the cost of curl and anticurl, and the pair rule on the moves), not from
  λ. That is the first exact check once the twist rules are fixed. Her answers of 19:12 to 19:50 UTC and the rules as
  equalities are in `docs/design/twist_curl_rules_2026-10-27.md`.

Update 135 (2026-10-28, 08:06 UTC by the clock): **DECISIONS (the author's, 28 October, 06:07 to 08:02 UTC): what is
whole is the total round a closed loop; links are scaffolding in 3D and absent in 2D; the light field on the links is
the carrier of electromagnetism, in 60° steps; one-sided loops use the published Möbius formula; the count stays
fixed.** Her words are in `sandbox/NOTEBOOK.md` under those times, verbatim; the rules as they stand are in
`docs/design/twist_curl_rules_2026-10-27.md`. Why this page changes: Update 134 left the twist rules as candidate forms;
a morning of her answers fixed several, two readings of the published mathematics were done
(`docs/reading/notes/2026-10-28_skyrmion_spin.md`, `..._linking_twist_writhe.md`), and one exact look decided the
carrier's step. Nothing here has been run as a test; the one look is in the sandbox lane (LOG, 28 October).

- **Her decisions, in order.** (1) The whole number is the total twist round a closed loop, not the twist on each
  piece (Călugăreanu's linking number; Dirac's whole number). (2) Twist can hold a photon's half curl, as charge; a
  +½ needs its −½, each half holds a photon, and the pair carries no charge; mass counts twist by size, charge by
  sign. (3) A gluon is an untwisted diagonal holding curl; a loop that cannot pay for a twist folds instead (*ours:*
  her fold is the published writhe, loop total = twist + fold; charge from twist, mass from |twist| + |fold|). (4)
  One-sided loops use the published Möbius form, linking = twist + 2 × fold, linking odd (Denne–Larsen 2023, read in
  part, proof to verify); so a neutral spin-½ object is a dark Möbius pair (*ours*). (5) The toy must allow
  electromagnetism in 3D: a carrier on the lattice, on the links, which are not real but are scaffolding in 3D
  spacetime and absent in 2D, where only twist and curl exist; a link is the unit of movement and the link count is
  distance. (6) The count stays fixed: "count is stable near mass". (7) The lattice's lengths are used (the box
  diagonal is √6). (8) The carrier's step is 60°; each ⅓ twist carries 60° of spin, three together 180° on the closed
  net-1 loop. (9) Gravity is curve in the same links (her 07:36 UTC), which is not yet reconciled with Update 40's
  quantity on the points; hers to settle before the gravity brief.
- **What exists already.** The carrier is the light field of Update 96 (`graphity.u1`, Q29): Wilson's phase on every
  link of a fixed graph, with its reproduction gate. Her 60° step restricts its phases to multiples of π/3; that is a
  change to make, with rule-6 tests, before any run with it. Riding the field on switching wiring is still unbuilt.
- **Exact, from the look** (`sandbox/link_carrier_dirac_wilson.py`): with an angle on every link of the 6 × 6 × 6
  torus, the flux out of every cube is 2π × a whole number (DeGrand–Toussaint); only whole charges couple to a closed
  loop single-valuedly, the proton's ⅔ + ⅔ − ⅓ = 1 passing as one loop (Dirac's rule, her (1)); a carrier with 180°
  steps cannot tell +1 from −1, so a half-curl carrier is blind to the sign of charge, while 60° steps separate −2 to
  2. *Exact (arithmetic):* three thirds of twist carry 3 × 60° = 180° of spin, one odd half turn, so by the ribbon
  rule the closed net-1 loop has spin ½ and charge 1; spin angle = ½ × net twist angle. *Exact, from the rules:* a
  loop of box diagonals closes with whole charge only when its thirds sum to a multiple of three (the published
  triality rule), allowing (⅔, ⅔, −⅓) = 1 and (⅔, −⅓, −⅓) = 0 and the meson pairs; a lone third never closes. *Ours:*
  the sheet's twist cost κ(1 − cos 2πτ) has Wilson's form, so the field on the links may be that term.
- **What fails or is silent, said plainly.** Mass by |twist| alone gives proton : electron = 5/3 against 1836, so
  almost all of the proton's mass must be fold (gluons), a number to compute (a look is running); chemistry needs the
  force the carrier would give and nothing follows yet; light on diagonals is slower than on axes (face √2/2, body
  √3/3, box √6/4 of the axis speed if a diagonal takes its link count in ticks), and a direction-dependent speed must
  be hidden to one part in 10¹⁷ (general knowledge, to verify).
- **Published relatives used** (read status in the two notes): Călugăreanu 1961 (read in part); Dirac 1931 (read in
  part); Dennis and Hannay 2005 (read in full); Denne–Larsen 2023 and Dagrosa et al. 2015 (read in part); Skyrme,
  Finkelstein–Rubinstein, Witten 1983 (abstract only or through reviews). Targets, never confirmations (rule 13).

Update 136 (2026-10-28, 13:05 UTC by the clock): **DECISIONS (the author's, 13:03 UTC): gravity's carrier is on the links, three-dimensional only, arriving with moving matter; the diagonals making the lattice rigid is a feature; the wiring is not fixed; the point count is constant in curved space and grows where curl meets flat space; the body diagonal is the axis through the triangle, not a side of it; spin reads the net twist round the loop (her confirmation, 12:09 UTC).** Her words are in `sandbox/NOTEBOOK.md` under those times. The link-rate picture needs the sign the other way, slower along the slowed diagonals near matter, which is what her Update 135 slowing already said.

The response rule is still owed. - **What follows at once** (*exact arithmetic, ours, under the sheet's mass = |twist| + |fold| in one unit; that fold weighs as twist does is our reading, not yet hers*): the proton must hold 1835.2 units of fold; the neutron, a proton holding one photon of −1 under her spin replacement (Update 138), makes a held photon 2.531 units, 1.293 MeV, which is the measured neutron–proton difference by construction; the charged pion, twist 0 with one photon, must hold 270.6 units of fold. These are what the loops must hold, not predictions. A prediction appears when a specific loop's fold is computed from its shape and compared.

The loops counted so far (`sandbox/proton_loop_fold.py`) hold at most 0.26 per diagonal, so a proton loop would need some 7,000 diagonals, a coil about 19 links across if packed; whether writhe grows with length on a packed coil of lattice diagonals is the question, exact and cheap.

Update 141 (2026-10-28, 22:55 UTC by the clock): **the author's reading of the free photon, and her rules for photons meeting matter.** Her words are in `sandbox/NOTEBOOK.md` at this time. Why this page changes: Update 138 left the free photon open under her spin replacement; she has given it.

- **The free photon (hers).** Half a curl moving along a body diagonal as a wave, spiralling forward at 45° round a loop of four cubes, one link per tick, the speed of light and the speed at which flat space opens; it costs nothing to pass because links are flexible in length; the link it is on lights up as the box diagonals round it twist and untwist for that moment (a quarter curl per two-cube, brief matter and antimatter forming); the spiral is smooth, not four right-angle turns.
- **Photons and matter (hers).** A closed surplus loop takes one photon and holds it as spin, which gives it charge; two photons hitting an open diagonal close it into a matter loop and an antimatter loop, which cancel at once and return the photons; a surplus loop holds one photon, a twisted loop a second as spin ("spin is always paid next"), and a further photon excites it.
- *Ours.* The free photon is not held, so under her replacement its charge is 0, and its spin reads from the spiral's full turn where matter's half turn reads ½: Update 138's open item closes. Her pair rule has the measured threshold in it by the unit: two photons of at least 0.511 MeV each make an electron and a positron (1.022 MeV; general knowledge, to verify). Her question, does a passing photon make heat: not in a lossless medium; the toy's light field (Update 96) conserves its energy at fixed wiring and the lattice only spreads a pulse; heat is curl left behind and needs an absorber, which is her excitation rule. One fact for her: in real atoms the excitation a photon causes is of the electron's orbit, not of the particle's own loop.
- **What can be tested in the toy:** whether the light field of Update 96 moves at one link per tick along a body diagonal direction (its measured top speed was one link per tick on the axes); whether a pulse passing a flat region leaves any curl behind (exact: none at fixed wiring); and, once a knot that is a dip exists, whether a pulse can be held by it.

Update 144 (2026-10-29, 00:35 UTC by the clock): **DECISION (the author's): a diagonal has twist only where it has curl; and the spectrum problem, the sharpest result of 28 October, with her three stability candidates checked.** Her words are in `sandbox/NOTEBOOK.md` at this time. Why this page changes: Update 143's option B (twist as a state on the diagonals) is narrowed by her to lengths derived from the edges with twist read from curl; and the rules as written, with one exact fact, predict a spectrum the world does not have.

- **The spectrum** (*exact, ours*): a closed loop of box diagonals is one colour, so its twisted diagonals share a sign and its charge is k/3, whole only for k a multiple of 3; with one curl per loop, k = 3 and every loop carries charge 1; with mass counting looped diagonals, the rules give a charged particle at every mass n × 0.170 MeV from n = 3 (0.51, 0.68, 0.85, 1.02 MeV, ...), and nothing charged exists between the electron and the muon at 105.7 MeV (621 diagonals). **A stability rule that forbids small loops other than the electron's is missing.** Her three candidates, checked the same hour: fold must be whole is killed (48 of the 3,456 simple non-planar five-loops have |fold| = 1.000 to three decimals, a particle at 0.85 MeV); a minimum coil puts the next object near 14 to 20 diagonals, about 3 MeV; the pyramid alone at four diagonals, 0.68 MeV. Hers to propose next.
- **The equation** (her question, "can't it be an equation yet?"): the record's one energy, with every term labelled exact, decided, ours or not built, is written out in `docs/reviews/2026-10-29_codex_audit_brief.md` for the next red-team pass, and that is the honest answer: one equation whose terms are of four kinds.
- **Kicked off overnight:** the seven-diagonal loops and their fold (`sandbox/proton_loop_fold.py 7`), for the stability question. Nothing toward "emergent three dimensions from the two-dimensional model" has a defined run yet.

Update 145 (2026-10-29, 10:46 UTC by the clock): **the author's corrections to Update 144 and her positions on the photon, the pyramid and the unit.** Her words are in `sandbox/NOTEBOOK.md` at this time. Why this page changes: one of the three stability candidates of Update 144 was ours miscounted, her photon rules now say what a photon does to the diagonals and to a massive loop, and she asks why masses are quoted in MeV.

- **The pyramid, corrected** (rule 11's form). Previous claim (ours, Update 144): the pyramid alone is a candidate small particle at four diagonals, 0.68 MeV. Failed because: the pyramid has no 1 × 1 × 2 diagonals, its sides being face diagonals and its axes body diagonals (exact, `sandbox/pyramid_check.py`; her correction), so under her rule that mass counts looped diagonals it has no mass. Replacement: none; it was never a candidate, and candidate (iii) is withdrawn. New test: none needed. What the pyramid is (hers): score-keeping, and the path light takes through a cube as a half curl that changes no energy, its links changing size with no effect. *Exact:* a diagonal, a (1, 1, 2) step, runs black to black and an anti-diagonal white to white, since the step changes x + y + z by an even number; the body diagonal (1, 1, 1) is the black-to-white axis.
- **The photon and the diagonals** (hers): a passing photon puts about a quarter twist and anti-twist on the 1 × 1 × 2 diagonals beside it, uncharged and temporary, cancelling to flat once the photon is more than two links on. A photon excites only a loop that already has mass (the closed −⅓, −⅓, −⅓); the first photon gives it its spin, and more photons excite it further. Photons are caught by the electron field and do not reach the nucleus. *Ours, not built:* the first is the rule the diagonals build would need (how a half curl on a body diagonal induces quarter twists beside it); its nearest published relative is vacuum polarization (general knowledge, to verify). *Ours, put to her:* if spin arrives with the first photon, the free electron's spin ½ needs a holder, and any spin rule must pass the three particles of O166 (neutron 0 and ½, charged pion 1 and 0, photon 0 and 1); the free electron is the fourth.
- **The smallest charged mass and the uncharged coil** (hers): the electron and anti-electron are the smallest charged mass; a coil can exist uncharged. *Ours, exact under the rules as written:* that makes the spectrum problem of Update 144 two-sided. A loop with no curl has no twist and no charge but keeps its mass (looped diagonals, twisted or not), so the rules give an uncharged object at every length as well as a charged one; the world's neutral particles below a GeV are the neutral pion (135 MeV) and the neutrinos (under an eV), and nothing at 0.68 to 3 MeV. The missing stability rule has to forbid both.
- **Why MeV** (her question). MeV is the unit the world quotes masses in (MeV/c², by E = mc²), so 0.511 MeV is the electron's mass and not an energy added to it; the toy's own numbers are counts (electron 3, muon 621, charged pion 819, proton 5,508, neutron 5,516 looped diagonals), and 0.170 MeV per diagonal is only the label the electron fixes. *Ours, the real point under her question:* in the toy a loop's energy is one curl per loop, whatever its length, while its mass is its length, so the toy has two numbers where the world has one (E = mc²); either each looped diagonal carries energy as well as mass, or the toy's energy and its mass are different quantities. Hers to decide; the record can state masses as counts from here if she prefers.
- **Addendum, 10:50 UTC: her reply on the electron's size and the uncharged coil** (hers, a hypothesis): the electron may hold more than three diagonals, since a three-diagonal loop of thirds may be too tight to close and need a gluon fold; spin is what lets a small loop hold its twist stably in less mass, so an uncharged electron is never seen. *Exact, ours:* three (1, 1, 2) steps do close on the lattice (flat triangles, fold 0), so 'too tight' is a rule about holding twist, not geometry. *Ours:* the unit then floats (0.511/n_e MeV per diagonal, every count scaling with n_e/3) and nothing in the toy pins n_e; 'no twist, no stability' would close the neutral side of the spectrum problem and leaves the charged side open.
- **Addendum, 10:53 UTC: her position on the two numbers** (hers): the toy's two numbers, a loop's energy (its curl) and its mass (its diagonal count), are the underlying pattern, and the world's one number is what hides it. *Ours:* this makes E = mc² a prediction for the toy rather than a definition (the two counts must stand in one ratio for every measured object, the electron first); the sealed runs conserve the curl and nothing yet conserves the count, so a rule for mass's conservation is owed; and claim 3's one quantity F is, in the toy, H alone, with mass a count F does not see.
- **Addendum, 10:59 UTC: charge as spin with twist, and spin alone as a second force** (hers, hypotheses): charge is spin with twist; spin with no twist is another force, probably what holds protons and neutrons together; the proton is a long cylinder whose loop fights itself by holding twist and anti-twist (⅔, ⅔, −⅓) to net a whole number, the neutron round it holding it together. *Exact:* ⅔ + ⅔ − ⅓ = 1. *Ours:* against O166's three particles the neutron and the photon fit and the charged pion (charge 1, spin 0) does not, so the rule as stated needs a reading of the pion before it stands; the standard strong force is independent of charge and the proton's measured form is close to round (general knowledge, to verify).
- **Addendum, 11:07 UTC: how a loop takes a photon's spin** (hers, a mechanism): a photon, half a curl with one unit of spin riding the spiral of four bicolour body diagonals, is spent on a loop's |⅓| segment, its curl stops, and the loop spins ½ in the direction of that twist; a third is the only segment a photon can act on, so an uncharged coil takes no spin. *Ours:* one unit of spin in and a half out needs the other half placed, or conservation breaks; a photon's energy is continuous in the world and half a curl is one amount in the toy, so the energy would have to sit in the spiral's pitch; a free electron cannot absorb a photon alone (general knowledge, to verify), which favours her 'excitation is of the orbit'. Not built.
- **Addendum, 11:09 UTC: what is conserved** (hers): spacetime holds only the net matter curl the burp left; anything made since is a pair, and the anti cancels with matter, so net matter stays the same. *Ours:* in the toy's terms this conserves net twist (matter minus anti), the counterpart of baryon and lepton number, and not the diagonal count; a single loop's size is still unfixed, so the spectrum problem stands apart from it; net twist is not yet a defined or conserved quantity in the toy.
- **Addendum, 11:10 UTC: DECISION (the author's): the pion's spin 0 is two opposed spins, a quark and an antiquark each with its twist.** The rule of this morning therefore reads: charge is twist carried by spin; spins can cancel while twists add. *Ours:* O166's three particles and the free electron all pass under it; this is the standard account of the pion (general knowledge, to verify); the next test is the rho meson, the same pair with spins parallel, whose mass (775 MeV, 4,550 diagonals against the pion's 819) the rules do not yet predict.
